This time,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Gov_{A}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{H}}}}}
is not well typed: the IFC requirements of Rules
[
typ-sel
]
and
[
typ-close
]
do not hold.

3.3
Type Preservation

Our type system guarantees by well typedness the usual correctness properties:
session fidelity
and
communication safety
.
The former states that a process correctly implements the session types assigned to its names, and the latter that no communication mismatches take place (such as simultaneous outputs on both names of a channel).

Both these properties follow directly from
type preservation
: well typedness is preserved across structural congruences (subject congruence;
Thm.

11
) and reduction (subject reduction;
Thm.

12
).
These results rely on two lemmas:

•

Lem.

9
states that names that are not free in a process are not assigned in the typing of the process.

•

Lem.

10
states that substitution in a process is reflected in its typing.

Lemma 9
.

Given
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma}}
, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\notin\fn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\notin\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.

Lemma 10

(Substitution)
.

Given
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:A{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P\{y/x\}}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}:A{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}.

Theorem 11

(Subject Congruence)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
.
The inductive cases correspond to closure under arbitrary process contexts in
Def.

1
; these cases follow from the IH straightforwardly.
The base cases correspond to the seven rules in
Fig.

5
(top).
In each case, we apply inversion on the typing of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
to derive the typing of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
, and vice versa, with straightforward reasoning about running secrecies.
The only interesting case is Rule [
sc-par-assoc
] (
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(P\mathbin{|}Q)\mathbin{|}R}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P\mathbin{|}(Q\mathbin{|}R)}}
), where we derive the running secrecy of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q\mathbin{|}R}}
from that of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P\mathbin{|}Q}}
, and vice versa.
The full proof is in the on
Appendix

A
.
∎

The following theorem states that (i) reduction preserves the well typedness of processes, and (ii) the running secrecy of processes may either stay the same or increase during reduction.
This implies that a process never forgets the secrets it has learned, but it may learn more secrets as it reduces.

Theorem 12

(Subject Reduction)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime}}}::\Gamma}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d^{\prime}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d\sqsubseteq d^{\prime}}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
.
The cases correspond to the reduction rules in
Fig.

5
(bottom).
In each case, we apply inversion on the typing of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
to derive the typing of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
.
The full proof is in the on
Appendix

A
; here, we show the interesting case of Rule [
red-send-recv
]:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)(x[a,b]\mathbin{|}y(z,w);P)}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P\{a/z,b/w\}}}
.
Given

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d\sqsubseteq d^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap d^{\prime}_{2}}},

(2)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 66.07237pt\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d^{\prime}_{1}\sqsubseteq c}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to170.73358pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-send}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime}_{1}}}::{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:A\mathbin{\otimes}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a}}:A^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}\end{array},

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d^{\prime\prime}_{2}=d^{\prime}_{2}\sqcup c}},

(4)

we have

\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{$\eqref{eq:subjRed:sendRecv1}$}\hskip 1.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.69054pt}\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{$\eqref{eq:subjRed:sendRecv2}$}\hskip 1.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.69054pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 9.07156pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{$\eqref{eq:subjRed:sendRecv3}$}\hskip 1.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.69054pt}\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{z}}:A^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}:B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to244.13388pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-recv}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y(z,w);P}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}:A^{\bot}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}}B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to454.18195pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-par}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 38.45726pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]\mathbin{|}y(z,w);P}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:A\mathbin{\otimes}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}:A^{\bot}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}}B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a}}:A^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 38.45726pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to377.26744pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.24812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize{{[{typ-res}]}}}}\vskip-0.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 125.582pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)(x[a,b]\mathbin{|}y(z,w);P)}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a}}:A^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}}\end{array}\Rightarrow\begin{array}[b]{@{}l@{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{$\text{\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{l:substitution} twice}$}\hskip 1.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to171.33368pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\scriptsize}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\;\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{${\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P\{a/z,b/w\}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a}}:A^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}$}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}}\end{array}

By assumption and by definition,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d^{\prime\prime}_{2}\sqsupseteq d^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
Also, by definition,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d^{\prime}_{2}\sqsupseteq d^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap d^{\prime}_{2}}}
, so, by assumption,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d^{\prime}_{2}\sqsupseteq d}}
.
Hence,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash d^{\prime\prime}_{2}\sqsupseteq d}}
.
∎

Liveness / Progress.

Liveness / Progress properties specify the conditions under which processes can reduce.
The progress property of APCP states that reduction takes place for a syntactic notion of “live” processes
[
28
]
.
Since this result does not rely on APCP’s priority mechanisms, it applies to our process language as well.

4
Logical Relation

This section defines an equivalence on typed processes up to “observable messages” (
Def.

21
) that we will use to state and prove DSNI in
Sec.

5
.
We first give some preliminary definitions in
Sec.

4.1
, before defining the logical relation that induces this equivalence in
Sec.

4.2
.

4.1
Preliminary Definitions

As anticipated in
Sec.

2.4
, we are interested in the behavior of a process when it runs in different contexts.
That is, we want to connect all the free names of the process in arbitrary ways.
To this end, we define evaluation contexts: processes with a hole inside which a process may reduce (so under parallel composition and restriction).
Evaluation contexts are typeset using an
orange and monospaced
font.

Definition 13

(Evaluation Context)
.

Evaluation contexts
(
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
) are defined as follows:

{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}::={\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{[\cdot]}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E\mathbin{|}P}}\mathbin{\mbox{\large{$\mid$}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu xy)E}}

We write
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}]}}
to denote the process obtained by replacing the hole
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{[\cdot]}}
in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
.

Any definitions on processes before and after this definition are lifted to evaluation contexts, without assigning any meaning to the hole.
The exception is that alpha renaming does not apply to names that are bound by restriction but not free inside the scope of the restriction.

Example 14
.

The following is an evaluation context:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu uw)(\nu xy)(\nu zv)(x();u[]\mathbin{|}z[]\mathbin{|}[\cdot])}}

Both
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{u}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}
are bound in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{u}}
appears free within the scope of the restriction as the subject of a close, alpha renaming applies:
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv_{\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu aw)(\nu xy)(\nu zv)(x();a[]\mathbin{|}z[]\mathbin{|}[\cdot])}}
.
However, the same does not hold for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}
:
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv_{\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu ua)(\nu xy)(\nu zv)(x();u[]\mathbin{|}z[]\mathbin{|}[\cdot])}}
.

We refer to the names that connect the process and its context as the
interface
.
Our logical relation focuses on messages between context and process, i.e., messages that must pass through the interface.
The following definition identifies outputs in process and context that are not blocked by prefixes. In particular,
\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})
is the set of names
along
which
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
is ready to output, and
\acon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}})
is the set of names
to
which the context is ready to output.

Definition 15

(Active Interface Names)
.

We define the set of
active output names
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
, denoted
\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})
, as the subjects of non-blocked outputs in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
:

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{0}})

\displaystyle:=\emptyset

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P\mathbin{|}Q}})

\displaystyle:=\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})\cup\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}})

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)P}})

\displaystyle:=\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})\setminus\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\}

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}})

\displaystyle:=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\}

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}})

\displaystyle:=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\}

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j}})

\displaystyle:=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\}

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x();P}})

\displaystyle:=\emptyset

\displaystyle\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x(y,z);P}})

\displaystyle:=\emptyset

\displaystyle\mathllap{\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x(z)\mathbin{\triangleright}\{i:P_{i}\}_{i\in I}}})}

\displaystyle:=\emptyset

We define the set of
active context output names
of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
, denoted
\acon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}})
, as the names in the interface of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
that are connected to active output names of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
through restriction:

\acon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}):=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\mid\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}},{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}}.~\big({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu xy)E^{\prime}}}\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\notin\fn({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}})\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\in\aon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}})\big)\}

We define the set of
active interface names
of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
as the union of the active context output names of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
and the active output names of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
:

\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}):=\acon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}})\cup\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})

Example 16
.

We illustrate the active interface names between
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y[]\mathbin{|}w();v();0}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
from
Example

14
.
It is easy to see that
\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\}
.
To determine
\acon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}})
we search for names in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
that are bound by restriction to names used for output, but not used themselves.
That is, we look for names in the interface between the context and the containing process, on which the containing process can expect to receive an output from the context.
For example, in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
, name
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{v}}
is bound to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{z}}
which is used for an output, while
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{v}}
itself is not used (it appears in the interface).
Since there are no further such names, we have
\acon({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{v}}\}
.
As such,
\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{v}}\}
.

The interface is where an attacker (cf.
Sec.

2.4
) may observe the behavior of our process.
In this, we assume that the attacker can only observe messages up to a certain secrecy level
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}
.
As such, our relation is only interested in the behavior of the process on
observable
channels.
To this end, we define a projection on typing contexts to filter out unobservable channels.
Also, we define when a process in context is well typed with respect to a given typing context of observable channels.

Definition 17

(Projection and Networks)
.

Given a secrecy lattice
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}
, a secrecy level
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}})
, and a typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
, we define the
projection

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\mathbin{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{(\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:A{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}})\mathbin{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{(\Gamma\mathbin{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:A{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}&\text{if ${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash c\sqsubseteq\xi}}$}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\mathbin{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}&\text{if ${\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega\Vdash c\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}$}\end{cases}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\emptyset\mathbin{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\emptyset}}

We often omit
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}
when it is clear from the context.

We say
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}

form a network with interface
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
observable up to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}
under
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}
, denoted
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
if and only if there are
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d}},{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma^{\prime}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma^{\prime}\mathbin{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime}}}::\Gamma^{\prime}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}]}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\emptyset}}
.
By abuse of notation, we write
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
to denote
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
and
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.

Example 18
.

We anticipate illustrating noninterference on the secure running example introduced in
Example

2
on a
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}
ow secrecy channel, by considering the projection of its typing context and an evaluation context to form a network.
Recall the typing and IFC annotations from
Examples

7
and
8
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Gov_{A}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{H}}}}}\mathbin{@}L}}::\Gamma^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}}}}:\&\{oc_{1}:\bot,oc_{2}:\bot\}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[L]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a_{I}}}:{\oplus}\{act:1,wait:1\}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[H]}}}}.

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma^{\prime}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}}}}:\&\{oc_{1}:\bot,oc_{2}:\bot\}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[L]}}}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu a_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{H}}}a_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}})(\nu a_{I}i_{A})(Gov_{A}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}}\mathbin{|}[\cdot]\mathbin{|}Int_{A})}}
.
It is straightforward to confirm that

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Gov_{A}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{H}}}}}]}}\mathbin{@}L}}::\emptyset}}
.
Hence,
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Gov_{A}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{H}}}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{L}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.

In our relation, we want to exhaust reductions on unobservable channels, after which we scrutinize behavior on observable names in the interface.
To this end, we define
unobservable reductions
, which entail reductions internal to the process or the context, but also communications between process and context on unobservable interface channels.

Definition 19

(Unobservable Reduction)
.

We define
unobservable reduction
as

{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}

if and only if
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}]}}
and
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.
We write
\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{?}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}
(resp.
\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{*}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}
) for the reflexive (resp. reflexive transitive) closure of
\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}
to denote that there are no
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}
.

Our relation often requires “zooming in” on specific parts of processes.
To this end, we define notions to deal with atomic parts of processes.

Definition 20

(Nodes and Normal Forms)
.

Given a process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
, we say
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
is a
node
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q\mathbin{|}R}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)Q}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{0}}
.

We say a process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu x_{i}y_{i})_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod_{j\in J}P_{j}}}
is in
normal form
if, for every
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{J}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{j}}}
is a node,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
is a
normal form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
.
Normal forms are closed under structural congruence: every process induces an equivalence class of structurally congruent normal forms.

Given a process in normal form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu x_{i}y_{i})_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\prod_{j\in J}P_{j}}}
, we define
\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}):=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{j}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{J}}\}
and
\binders({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}):=\big\{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x_{i}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y_{i}}}\}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{i}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{I}}\big\}
.
By abuse of notation, given a process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
not necessarily in normal form, we write
\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}})
to denote
\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}})
for an arbitrary normal form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
.

For example, let

\displaystyle\hskip-4.30554pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu uw)\big((\nu xy)(z();x[]\mathbin{|}y();u[])\mathbin{|}w();0\big)\mathbin{|}0}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu uw)(\nu xy)(z();x[]\mathbin{|}y();u[]\mathbin{|}w();0)}}.

Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}
is a normal form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
, with
\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{z();x[]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();u[]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w();0}}\}
and
\binders({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}})=\{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{u}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}\},\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\}\}
.

4.2
The Relation

Having presented all its ingredients, we now introduce our logical relation.
As usual, the relation consists of two parts: a
term interpretation
and a
value interpretation
, defined by mutual multiset induction on the interfaces of processes.
The term interpretation is the main part of the relation, and is responsible for calling on the value interpretation when a message is ready to be communicated across the observable interface, as well as ensuring deadlock sensitivity of our noninterference result.
The value interpretation zooms in on the interface, and ensures that the two runs of the process behave identically on observable messages that are to be communicated across the interface.

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket\iff

(5)

\displaystyle\wedge({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})

(6)

\displaystyle\wedge\forall{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}.~{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{*}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.375pt\kern-4.88191pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.375pt\kern-4.88191pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.36249pt\kern-4.23051pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.6875pt\kern-3.03471pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}

(7)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}\wedge{}}\implies\exists{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}.~\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{*}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.375pt\kern-4.88191pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.375pt\kern-4.88191pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.36249pt\kern-4.23051pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.6875pt\kern-3.03471pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}\\
\wedge\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\big(\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})\cup\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\big)\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}).~({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathrlap{\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket}\\
\wedge\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})=\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})\end{array}

Figure 7:
Term relation.

Let us start by presenting our term interpretation, denoted
\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
, in
Fig.

7
.
It relates pairs of processes, given a secrecy lattice
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}
, a secrecy level
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}})
, and an interface
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
.
We break down its definition part by part.
Part (
5
) implicitly requires each process to be separable into a context
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{i}}}
and a process
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{i}}}
.
Part (
6
) then requires the interface between
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{i}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{i}}}
to correspond to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
up to observability
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}
(cf.
Def.

17
).
Part (
7
) exhausts unobservable reductions for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}
(cf.
Def.

19
) in every way possible, resulting in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Part (
7
) first requires
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}
to “catch up” through exhaustive unobservable reductions, resulting in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
Then, Part (
7
) invokes the value interpretation, presented next, to scrutinize any messages that are ready to be transferred across the observable part of the interface of either
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{i}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{i}}}
(cf.
Def.

15
).
Finally, Part (
7
) ensures deadlock sensitivity by requiring the observable messages of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
to coincide.
In particular, if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
does not produce any observable messages along a name in the interface due to a deadlock imposed by a secret, Part (
7
) guarantees that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
does not produce any observable messages on that name either.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{1}}\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:1{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}\rrbracket\iff\big(({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:1{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})

\displaystyle\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{1}}}\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{2}}}\wedge({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}\big[x[]\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\big]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}\big[x[]\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}\big]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket\big)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\oplus}}}\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:{\oplus}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}\rrbracket\iff

(11)

\displaystyle\hphantom{{}\wedge{}}({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:{\oplus}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})

(12)

\displaystyle\wedge\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{I}}.~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j}}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}})\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j}}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})

(13)

\displaystyle\wedge\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}l@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})\implies&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{2}}}\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{1}}}\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{2}}}\\
&{}\wedge{}({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}\big[x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\big]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}\big[x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}\big]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}}}\rrbracket\end{array}

(14)

\displaystyle\wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\notin\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})\implies\big({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{2}}}\notin\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})

(15)
