See
31

Proof.

Suppose, toward a contradiction, that none of these three items hold.
Then the only possibility is that
Lem.

30
applies, on names of which at least one is in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
.
W.l.o.g., assume we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)(x[]\mathbin{|}y();Q)}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}]}}
.
At least one of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}
appears in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
, so at least one of the close on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
and the wait on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}
appears in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
; w.l.o.g., assume the wait on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}
appears in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}
and the restriction and close on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
appear in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}
.
We then have that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu xy)(x[]\mathbin{|}G)}}
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{G}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{H[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();Q}}]}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{H[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q}}]}}
.
By the assumption,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma^{\prime}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma^{\prime}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
.
However,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}}\mathbin{@}d}}::\Gamma^{\prime}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}
, and thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{(\Gamma^{\prime}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}})\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}
.
Clearly, then
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}}})\notin\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.
This violates the definition of
\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}
(
Def.

19
): a contradiction.
∎

See
32

Proof.

By the assumption, we know the typing of all processes involved (cf.
Def.

17
).
Moreover, there are normal forms
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\star}_{2}}}
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}
respectively, such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\star}_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
(cf.
Def.

28
).
This means that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\star}_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
are equivalent, up to the ordering of binders and nodes, and
\alpha
-renaming.
For a smoother proof, we thus obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\star}_{2}}}
by rearranging binders and nodes, and
\alpha
-renaming, such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\star}_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
(i.e.,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
is still a normal form of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}
) and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
.
By
Def.

20
, it suffices to consider only the normal forms
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
instead of their counterparts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}
: we show that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
in normal form implies
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{?}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}}
in normal form and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
, from which the thesis follows by definition.

By
Lem.

31
, we can distinguish three cases from which
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
follows.

•

(Internal in context:
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
)

Then the thesis holds directly with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
.

•

(Internal in process:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}
)

By
Lem.

30
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
’s reduction is due to one of three possible synchronizations inside some evaluation context.
Note that
Lem.

30
may give us processes that are
\alpha
-variant to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
; in the following we implicitly apply further
\alpha
-renaming to match the names in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
We consider all three possibilities separately.

–

(Close-Wait)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)(x[]\mathbin{|}y();R)}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\quasi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}}\mathbin{@}d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}}::{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:1{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\sqcup c}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\quasi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();R}}\mathbin{@}d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}::\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}:\bot{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}\sqcup c}}
.

We distinguish cases on whether or not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
(cf.
Def.

27
).

*

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
)

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is bound in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
, the inclusion of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}}
in
\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
must be because it was added through the inductive clause of
Def.

27
.
We must then have
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\}\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();R}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.
Then also
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\}\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
and
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();R}}\}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
.
Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)(x[]\mathbin{|}y();R)}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}}]}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}}
.

To conclude the proof, we need to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}}]}}
; it suffices to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
, but now both relevant forms agree on bound names.
We have to show that
\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})=\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
and
\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})=\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.
Both directions of both set inclusions are analogous, so we only detail
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})\implies b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
and
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})\implies n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.
The analysis follows by the inductive definition of
\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})
and
\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})
(cf.
Def.

27
).

In the base case, we have some
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})
added through the interface.
The analysis depends on whether
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
or
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
.

·

If
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
, then
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.
Hence,
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
, so
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}}})
.
It follows that
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.

·

If
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
, it is immediate that
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.

In the inductive case, we have some
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})
added through a binder
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})
connected to some
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})
added in a previous step.
The analysis depends on whether
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
or
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
.

·

If
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
, it must be that
b\in\binders({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
.
The analysis depends on whether
n^{\prime}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
or
n^{\prime}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
.

If
n^{\prime}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
, then
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.
Hence,
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
.
Then also
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
and
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
, so
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
and
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
.
This means that
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}}})
and
b\in\binders({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}}})
.
Hence,
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
and
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.

If
n^{\prime}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x();R}}\in N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
was added through some
b^{\prime}\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})\cap\binders({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
.
Hence,
b^{\prime}\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})\cap\binders({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}}})
, adding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x();R}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
.
Therefore,
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.
In conclusion,
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
and
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.

·

If
n\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
, the analysis depends on whether
b\in\binders({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
or
b\in\binders({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
.

If
b\in\binders({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}}})
, then
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
to add
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x();R}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
via
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})\setminus\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
.
Then
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})
and
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})\setminus\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
, so
n^{\prime}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{2}}})
.
Hence,
n^{\prime}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
, and so
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
and
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.

If
b\in\binders({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}})
, it follows immediately that
n\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
and
b\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}})
.

*

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}}\notin\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
)

This may be due to either of two reasons.
The first possibility is that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
: this would directly violate the requirements for relevancy in
Def.

27
.
The second possibility is that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();R}}\notin\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
: otherwise, we can follow the reasoning above to contradict the assumption that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]}}\notin\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.
We show that in the former case, the latter case holds as well; i.e., either way,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();R}}\notin\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.

Assuming
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\sqcup c\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
, either
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
or
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{c\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
, since Rule [
typ-close
] demands
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\sqsubseteq c}}
, also
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{c\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
.
Hence, we always have
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{c\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}\sqcup c\not\sqsubseteq\xi}}
, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y();R}}\notin\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.

By Rule [
typ-wait
],
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}}\mathbin{@}e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}::\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}
.
It follows by Rule [
typ-par
] that none of the nodes in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}}
have quasi-running secrecy
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\sqsubseteq\xi}}
.
Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R}}
cannot affect relevancy of nodes of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
.
Hence, the thesis holds with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
.

–

(Send-Receive)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu xy)(x[a,b]\mathbin{|}y(w,z);R)}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{F_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{R\{a/w,b/z\}}}]}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\quasi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}}\mathbin{@}d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}}::{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}:A\mathbin{\otimes}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a}}:A^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\sqcup c}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{e_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}:={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\quasi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y(z,w);R}}\mathbin{@}d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}}}::\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}:A^{\bot}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}}B^{\bot}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{d_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}}\sqcup c}}
.

We distinguish cases on whether or not
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.

*

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
)

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is bound in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{a}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}\notin\fcn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}})
, this inclusion can only hold through the inductive clause in
Def.

27
.
Hence,
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}\}\in\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y(w,z);R}}\in\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
.
The rest follows as above.

*

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[a,b]}}\notin\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})
)

Analogous to the case above.

–

(Select-Branch)

Analogous to the case above.

•

(Communication between context and process on names not in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
)

By definition, the secrecy levels of the involved names are incomparable to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}
.
Therefore, none of the nodes involved are relevant or influence relevancy of any other nodes:
\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})=\N({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}})
and
\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})=\B({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{1}}})
.
Hence, the thesis holds with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q^{\prime}_{2}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
.
∎

See
29

Proof.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{2}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}
.
Take any
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime}_{1}}}::\emptyset}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime}_{2}}}::\emptyset}}
.
We need to show that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
, which we do by induction on
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.

The first condition is that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
; this holds by assumption.

Next, take any
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{1}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{*}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}
.
A straightforward induction on the length of these unobservable reductions shows that, by
Def.

19
and
Lem.

32
, there are
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P_{2}}}\mathrel{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\longrightarrow}}}^{*}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longrightarrow}}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}}
,
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\@netw^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
.

Now, we need to show that, for every
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\big(\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})\cup\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\big)\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
,

({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket.

Take any such
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
.
Either
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})
or
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})
; w.l.o.g., assume the former.
The rest of the analysis depends on the type of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}
.

First, we discuss the output-like cases (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\oplus}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\mathbin{\otimes}}}
).
In each case, by well-typedness,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is the subject of an output-like prefix in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})
, this prefix is unguarded.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})=\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{1}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}})
, the node in which the prefix appears is relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Therefore, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
, there is also a relevant node in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
where this prefix appears unguarded.

•

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{1{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
)

Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[]\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
.
Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\Gamma_{1}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma_{2}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}\big[x[]\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\big]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\emptyset}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}\big[x[]\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\big]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\emptyset}}
.
Also clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{1}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{2}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}
.
Hence, since
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
, it follows from the IH that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}\big[x[]\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\big]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}\big[x[]\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\big]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}\rrbracket
.
This proves that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
.

•

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\oplus}\{i:A_{i}\}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
)

Then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{I}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j}}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j}}\in\nodes({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})
.
The analysis depends on whether
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
or not.

–

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
)

By well-typedness,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\in\fn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})\cap\fn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{2}}}
.
Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
.
Similar to the case above, and since
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
, it follows from the IH that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}\big[x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\big]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}\big[x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\big]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}\rrbracket
.
This proves that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
.

–

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\notin\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
)

By well-typedness,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu b_{1}b^{\prime})(x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{1})}}
.
The selection on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is a relevant node of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
, it is also a relevant node of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
Moreover,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{2}}}\notin\fn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})
: otherwise,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{2}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}
, and then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{1}}}\in\fn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})
.
Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu b_{2}b^{\prime})(x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{2})}}
.
Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\Gamma_{1}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma_{2}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}\big[(\nu b_{1}b^{\prime})(x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})\big]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\emptyset}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}\big[(\nu b_{2}b^{\prime})(x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}})\big]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\emptyset}}
.
Again, since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
, the chain of nodes and binders that are relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
through the binder
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu b_{1}b^{\prime})}}
has an equivalent such chain in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
through
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu b_{2}b^{\prime})}}
and the selection on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b_{2}}}
.
Hence, the effect on relevant nodes and binders by removing the binder and the selection on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is the same on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
as it is on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
.
Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{1}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}\mathbin{\Downarrow}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma_{2}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}}}
.
Also,
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{A_{j}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\oplus}\{A_{i}\}_{i\in I}}})
, so
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.
It then follows from the IH that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}\big[(\nu b_{1}b^{\prime})(x[b_{1}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})\big]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}\big[(\nu b_{2}b^{\prime})(x[b_{2}]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}})\big]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b^{\prime}}}}}\rrbracket
.
This proves that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
.

•

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{A\mathbin{\otimes}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
)

Analogous to the previous case.

Next, we discuss the negative cases (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\bot}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\text{{\&}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}}}}
).
In each case, by well-typedness,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is the subject of an input-like prefix in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
The context
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}
binds
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
to some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}
by restriction, and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}
contains a complementary output-like prefix on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{y}}
.
Following similar reasoning, the same holds for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\ain({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})
, this output-like prefix appears unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
To prove the thesis, we assume that this prefix also appears unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}}
.

•

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\bot{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
)

We have
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu yx)(y[]\mathbin{|}E^{\prime\prime}_{1})}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu yx)(y[]\mathbin{|}E^{\prime\prime}_{2})}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu yx)(y[]\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{1})}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu yx)(y[]\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{2})}}
.
Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\Gamma_{1}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma_{2}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime\prime}_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\emptyset}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime\prime}_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\emptyset}}
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
denote the nodes of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
, respectively, in which
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
appears.
To prove that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
, it suffices to show that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
and any related binders are relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
if and only if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
and any related binders are relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
; any connected nodes/binders follow similar reasoning.
We detail only the left-to-right direction; the other direction is analogous.
Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
is relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\quasi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}})\sqsubseteq\xi}}
, and thus
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
is also relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
through
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
in the interface.
Then also
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
is relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\quasi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}})\sqsubseteq\xi}}
.
The analysis depends on how
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
is relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
: (i) through the interface, or (ii) through a restriction with another relevant node.
In case (i), it follows straightforwardly that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
is also relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
In case (ii), the connected node is also relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
, and hence there is a related node that is also relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
Since the two processes agree on observable channels, the channel responsible for including
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{1}}}
as a relevant node of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}
is also bound in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
.
Then we can conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{Q_{2}}}
is a relevant node of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
.

Since
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
, it then follows from the IH that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime\prime}_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime\prime}_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}}\rrbracket
.
This proves that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
.

•

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\text{{\&}}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
)

We have
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})(\nu yx)(y[b]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}E^{\prime\prime}_{1})}}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{E^{\prime}_{2}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})(\nu yx)(y[b]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}E^{\prime\prime}_{2})}}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{j}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{I}}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu yx)(y[b]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{1})}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}:={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{(\nu yx)(y[b]\mathbin{\triangleleft}j\mathbin{|}P^{\prime}_{2})}}
.
Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\Gamma_{1}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:A_{j}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\Gamma_{2}\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:A_{j}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})E^{\prime\prime}_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}}}::\emptyset}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})E^{\prime\prime}_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}]}}\mathbin{@}d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}}}::\emptyset}}
.

It is clear that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}\notin\fcn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}})\cup\fcn({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}})
; hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}
plays no role in relevancy in either process.
Therefore, proving that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}
is analogous to the previous case.
Since
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{A_{j}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\text{{\&}}\{i:A_{i}\}_{i\in I}}})
, we have
\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:A_{j}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}})<\varpi({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.
Therefore, by the IH,
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})E^{\prime\prime}_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}]}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})E^{\prime\prime}_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}]}})\in\mathsf{E}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma\setminus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{b}}:A_{j}{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}\rrbracket
.
This proves that
({\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})E^{\prime\prime}_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{1}}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.58,0}\mathtt{(\nu bb^{\prime})E^{\prime\prime}_{2}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime\prime}_{2}}})\in\mathsf{V}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\Omega}};{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}}\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}}\rrbracket
.

•

(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\textstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\text{{\&}}}$}}}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{[c]}}}}
)

Analogous to the previous case.

Finally, we show that
\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})=\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.
To prove this set equality, we take any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
and prove that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
; the other direction is analogous.
Clearly,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}
is the subject of an output-like prefix in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
, this output-like prefix must appear unguarded in a node in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
If the quasi-running secrecy of this node is observable, this node is relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{\equiv}}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\mathit{\xi}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
must also have a relevant node in which the output-like prefix appears unguarded.
Otherwise, the node is not relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{1}}}
, and hence the node in which the output-like prefix appears in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
is also not relevant in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}}
.
Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{x}}\in\aon({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{P^{\prime}_{2}}})\cap\dom({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\mathsf{\Gamma}})
.
∎
